=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given two strings, str1 and str2, of lengths n and m, respectively.
A string word of length n + m - 1 is defined to be generated by str1 and str2 if it satisfies the following conditions for each index 0 <= i <= n - 1:

If str1[i] == 'T', the substring of word with size m starting at index i is equal to str2, i.e., word[i..(i + m - 1)] == str2.
If str1[i] == 'F', the substring of word with size m starting at index i is not equal to str2, i.e., word[i..(i + m - 1)] != str2.

Return the lexicographically smallest possible string that can be generated by str1 and str2. If no string can be generated, return an empty string "".
 
Example 1:

Input: str1 = "TFTF", str2 = "ab"
Output: "ababa"
Explanation:
The table below represents the string "ababa"



Index
T/F
Substring of length m


0
'T'
"ab"


1
'F'
"ba"


2
'T'
"ab"


3
'F'
"ba"



The strings "ababa" and "ababb" can be generated by str1 and str2.
Return "ababa" since it is the lexicographically smaller string.

Example 2:

Input: str1 = "TFTF", str2 = "abc"
Output: ""
Explanation:
No string that satisfies the conditions can be generated.

Example 3:

Input: str1 = "F", str2 = "d"
Output: "a"

 
Constraints:

1 <= n == str1.length <= 10^4
1 <= m == str2.length <= 500
str1 consists only of 'T' or 'F'.
str2 consists only of lowercase English characters.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def generateString(self, str1: str, str2: str) -> str:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Pseudo:
def build_transition(pat):
    m=len(pat)
    pi=[0]*m
    for i in range(1,m):
        j=pi[i-1]
        while j>0 and pat[i]!=pat[j]:
            j=pi[j-1]
        if pat[i]==pat[j]:
            j+=1
        pi[i]=j
    trans = [[0]*26 for _ in range(m+1)]
    for state in range(m+1):
        for ci in range(26):
            c = chr(97+ci)
            if state == m:
                # fallback to pi[m-1] before processing
                s = pi[m-1]
            else:
                s = state
            while s > 0 and pat[s] != c:
                s = pi[s-1]
            if pat[s] == c:
                s += 1
            trans[state][ci] = s
    return trans, pi
But for state m, if m=0? no, m>=1. pat[s] valid for s<=m-1 after fallback. Good.

for q in range(m+1):

     q2 = trans[q][c]
     pre[q2] |= 1<<q
Then dp_cur = OR pre[q'] for q' in base.

x = base

    lsb = x & -x
    idx = lsb.bit_length() - 1
    dp_cur |= pre[idx]
    x ^= lsb
dp[p] = dp_cur
dp_next = dp_cur

    for ci in range(26):

        trans[state][ci] = s

class Solution:
    def generateString(self, str1: str, str2: str) -> str:
        n = len(str1)
        m = len(str2)
        L = n + m - 1
        
        # prefix function
        pi = [0]*m
        for i in range(1,m):
            j = pi[i-1]
            while j > 0 and str2[i] != str2[j]:
                j = pi[j-1]
            if str2[i] == str2[j]:
                j += 1
            pi[i] = j
        
        # transitions
        trans = [[0]*26 for _ in range(m+1)]
        for state in range(m+1):
            for ci in range(26):
                c = chr(97+ci)
                if state == m:
                    s = pi[m-1]
                else:
                    s = state
                while s > 0 and str2[s] != c:
                    s = pi[s-1]
                if str2[s] == c:
                    s += 1
                trans[state][ci] = s
        
        # preimage_all
        pre = [0]*(m+1)
        for q in range(m+1):
            bitq = 1 << q
            for ci in range(26):
                q2 = trans[q][ci]
                pre[q2] |= bitq
        
        full_mask = (1 << (m+1)) - 1
        allowed = [full_mask] * L
        t_bit = 1 << m
        f_mask = full_mask ^ t_bit
        for p in range(m-1, L):
            i = p - m + 1
            if str1[i] == 'T':
                allowed[p] = t_bit
            else:
                allowed[p] = f_mask
        
        dp = [0]*(L+1)
        dp[L] = full_mask
        dp_next = full_mask
        for p in range(L-1, -1, -1):
            base = dp_next & allowed[p]
            cur = 0
            x = base
            while x:
                lsb = x & -x
                idx = lsb.bit_length() - 1
                cur |= pre[idx]
                x ^= lsb
            dp[p] = cur
            dp_next = cur
        
        if (dp[0] & 1) == 0:
            return ""
        
        res = []
        q = 0
        for p in range(L):
            allowed_after = allowed[p]
            next_dp = dp[p+1]
            for ci in range(26):
                q2 = trans[q][ci]
                if (allowed_after >> q2) & 1 and (next_dp >> q2) & 1:
                    res.append(chr(97+ci))
                    q = q2
                    break
            else:
                return ""  # should not happen
        return ''.join(res)

```python
class Solution:
    def generateString(self, str1: str, str2: str) -> str:
        n = len(str1)
        m = len(str2)
        L = n + m - 1

        # KMP prefix function
        pi = [0] * m
        for i in range(1, m):
            j = pi[i - 1]
            while j > 0 and str2[i] != str2[j]:
                j = pi[j - 1]
            if str2[i] == str2[j]:
                j += 1
            pi[i] = j

        # KMP automaton transitions
        trans = [[0] * 26 for _ in range(m + 1)]
        for state in range(m + 1):
            for ci in range(26):
                c = chr(97 + ci)
                if state == m:
                    s = pi[m - 1]
                else:
                    s = state
                while s > 0 and str2[s] != c:
                    s = pi[s - 1]
                if str2[s] == c:
                    s += 1
                trans[state][ci] = s

        # pre[q2] = bitset of states q that can reach q2 by some character
        pre = [0] * (m + 1)
        for q in range(m + 1):
            bit = 1 << q
            for ci in range(26):
                pre[trans[q][ci]] |= bit

        full_mask = (1 << (m + 1)) - 1
        t_bit = 1 << m
        f_mask = full_mask ^ t_bit

        allowed = [full_mask] * L
        for p in range(m - 1, L):
            i = p - m + 1
            if str1[i] == 'T':
                allowed[p] = t_bit
            else:
                allowed[p] = f_mask

        # dp[p] = bitset of KMP states possible before processing position p
        dp = [0] * (L + 1)
        dp[L] = full_mask
        nxt = full_mask

        for p in range(L - 1, -1, -1):
            base = nxt & allowed[p]
            cur = 0
            x = base
            while x:
                lsb = x & -x
                idx = lsb.bit_length() - 1
                cur |= pre[idx]
                x ^= lsb
            dp[p] = cur
            nxt = cur

        if (dp[0] & 1) == 0:
            return ""

        res = []
        q = 0
        for p in range(L):
            for ci in range(26):
                q2 = trans[q][ci]
                if (allowed[p] >> q2) & 1 and (dp[p + 1] >> q2) & 1:
                    res.append(chr(97 + ci))
                    q = q2
                    break

        return "".join(res)
```
